2 .    R E C O G N I T I O N        
 A.  Heather Kelly, Art Prize 2011 Grand Rapids  
This year, artwork by Troy High art teacher Heather Kelly, was chosen for display 
at Art Prize in Grand Rapids.   Art Priz e is a glorious exam ple to people who 
believe that art has the power to inspir e and enliven communities, and is one of 
the country’s most prestigious annual art exhibitions. 
Ms. Kelly’s piece entitled “Wish Project” is a mixed media work inspired by 
“idealized motherhood.” 
We’d like to play a portion of an interv iew Ms. Kelly did regarding this piece. 
(play interview) 
Heather, would you please come forward and share a bit about your art project? 
Recommended Action:  Play interview and present certificate to Heather.

2. B.    11.01.11 
2 .    R E C O G N I T I O N        
 B.  Rebecca Brewer “2011 ING National Unsung Heroes®” Award 
Rebecca Brewer, an advance placement bi ology teacher at Troy High School, was 
recognized as the nation’s most innova tive educator in the 2011 ING Unsung 
Heroes® Awards Program.  Through th is program, ING awards educators 
throughout the United States  grants for innovative te aching ideas. Rebecca was 
presented with a check for $27,000 to help  fund her award-winning project, “Mini-
Med School” at Troy High School. 
“Mini-Med School: Simulating Surgeries,” is designed to move 11th and 12th grade 
biology students from simply dissecting preserved specimens to also performing 
mock surgical procedures on the organs. 
Congratulations, Mrs. Brewer!  Please come forward to be recognized. 
_______________________________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Present certificate.

C. Healthier U.S. School Challenge Award (HUSSC) 
Tonight we recognize our Food Serv ice Department fo r the successful 
accomplishment of attaining the Bronze Award for the Healthier US School 
Challenge, which is a tremendous achi evement on its own since all twelve 
schools earned the award simultaneously.  At this  time, only two school 
districts in the state of Michigan; Tr oy School District and Birmingham 
Public Schools, have had all elementary schools qu alify for this award.  
Both districts partner with the Sodexo Corporation.

Advanced Placement Honors 
Troy students were honored for their outstanding 
performance on Advanced Placement exams during the 
past school year.  Troy st udents were honored by the 
College Board in four award categories: AP Scholar, AP 
Scholar with Honors, AP Sc holar with Distinction and 
National AP Scholar.  The Board was asked to recognize 
the students who have earned the National AP Scholar 
honors.  This award is grante d to students who receive an 
average grade of four on all of the AP exams that were 
taken last year.  Students in this category must also take 
eight or more AP exams during the school year.  A number 
of those students were seniors last year.  Students who 
achieved this honor during their junior year were invited 
to be present at this evening’s meeting.  Those students 
recognized were: Sandeep Arakali, David Bai, Neha Bokil, 
Allen Chen, Rolland He, Darwin Hsu, Yujing Liang, and 
Clark Zhang.  Only one student was present at to receive

Nicholas Green Distinguished 
Student Award 
Elizabeth Brooks, chair of the scholarship committee of 
Michigan Association for Gifted Children (MAGC) came to 
tonight’s Board meeting to present a scholarship for the 
Nicholas Green Distinguished Student Awards.  The 
Nicholas Green Foundation was established by Maggie and 
Reg Green to honor the memory of their seven-year-old 
son, Nicholas, who was killed in a drive-by shooting while 
visiting Italy in 1994.  The Greens paired with National 
Association for Gifted Children NAGC to fund this awards 
program, which is designed to recognize distinguished 
achievement in academics, leadership, or the arts, in 
children grades 3 through 6.  Each year, one child in each 
state may be named a Nicholas Green Distinguished 
Student.  Each winner receiv es a $500 U.S. savings bond 
and an NAGC Certificate of Excellence.  This year, Brittany 
Ngia, a sixth grade Boulan Park Middle School student 
received this award.   
A five-minute recess was prov ided in order to allow 
members of the audience to depart during this time.

2011-2012 School Year 
Anne Mull gave the Board an overview of professional 
activities and learning opportunities that have been 
offered to both teachers and administrators.  She 
mentioned that the administrators had a retreat in August 
at the Bloomfield Hills Nature Center.  A few of the areas 
they covered dealt with iPad technology, collaboration and 
distance learning.  She talked about secondary vertical 
and elementary grade level meetings and the standards of 
mathematical practice.  She mentioned that the District 
adopted new ELA materials for middle school from Holt-
McDougal, which offers online resources to allow work in 
lab-type settings.  She also talked about Atlas Rubicon, 
which is a tool the District is currently working on to 
manage curriculum online so that it can be shared among 
all teachers throughout the District.  She mentioned 
Inform, which is a data management system used for 
assessing data in what ch ildren must learn so that 
teachers can apply intervention methods to students who 
need additional help and also provide more opportunities 
for the gifted students.   
Mrs. Mull also mentioned what the District has been doing 
in the area of diversity and inclusion and that they will be 
introducing a book written by Gary Howard called “We 
Can’t Teach what We Don’t Know”, which is about white 
teachers in multiracial school s.  She explained that the 
District has purchased copies of this book for members of 
the Diversity Council, administ rators and several for each 
building.  She also discussed some of the after school 
professional development opportunities that will focus on 
smart boards and iPad technology, bullying, effective 
grading practices, and college/work place readiness, which 
will be a big part of conversations in the future.   
Mrs. Mull shared that the next team visit for NCA 
accreditation will be in the fall of 2014.   She also 
reviewed that the District will be hosting a Student 
Learning Showcase again this year and that invitations 
would be coming soon.  She discussed Visible Thinking to 
raise cognitive skills and promote inquiry in the classroom.  
She talked about common core standards and mentioned 
the new Troy Educator Appraisal Model.  She talked briefly 
about Title I schools and that two buildings were 
discontinued and that two new ones were added.  She 
stated that Troy is truly a professional learning community 
and that teachers and principals have so much expertise 
to share and understand what good instruction means in 
the classroom.

Troy Board of Education Meeting 6 October 4, 2011 
21
(TEAM) 
September 26
th professional development session.  Mrs. 
Rich Machesky introduced a new program that was 
the second semester of the 2010-2011 school year, a 
committee comprised of eight teachers and eight 
administrators was formed to create a new evaluation tool 
that would comply with Michigan’s new education reform 
legislation.  Through collabo ration and hard work the 
group developed a model showing a rubric that will be 
used in evaluation of teachers and administrators.  Dr. 
Machesky together with Karen Bush, third grade teacher 
at Barnard Elementary, shared a video presentation with 
the Board regarding the new Troy Educator Appraisal 
Model (TEAM).  Karen stated that links and resources for 
this process and rubrics would be made available online 
for teachers and administrators  to become familiar with.  
She stated that the overall goal of this mission is how to 
help teachers become more effective in the classroom.  
She shared that using this instrument will produce quality 
evaluations and stated that the development of “TEAM” 
was a huge undertaking.  She said that the collaboration 
and teamwork of the administration and teachers was very 
positive and rewarding.   She reviewed the rubric system 
and said that teachers will be asked to create their own 
goals including the goal of student growth, which is the 
main focus.  Dr. Machesky shared that administrators will 
meet with each teacher in their building for approximately 
30-45 minutes to discuss their individual goals and give 
them time to self-reflect and identify areas they want to 
improve upon.  He commented that an alternate plan 
would need to be discussed fo r evaluating itinerants who 
work in multiple buildings. He added that it will be 
important for administrators to  support their teachers so 
that they are successful in helping their students grow.  
He also stated that admini strators will have much more 
time dedicated to the evaluation process and classroom 
observations.   
time and the effort they put into developing this new 
evaluation tool.   
Mr. Miletti also congratulated the committee on their 
efforts and that they did a great job on the video.  He also 
asked if the video could be put on the website as a link for 
t h e  p u b l i c  t o  a c c e s s .   H e  a lso inquired as to how the 
District is measuring student growth.  Dr. Machesky 
replied that it is a difficulty school districts struggle with.  
They need to decide what student growth means and 
what makes sense.  He stated that teachers know their 
students best and they are trying to identify and track 
their growth in subjects both at the beginning of the year 
and end of the year.

Legislative Impact on School 
presentation explaining the b ills that have been recently 
introduced into the Michigan Legislature and the effect 
they will have on school funding.  He discussed the history 
of the Adopted School Aid Act and the $470 permanent 
reduction of the foundation allowance per student, which 
has resulted in a permanent loss of revenue for the 2011-
2012 fiscal year. Mr. Rajter also shared that a one-time 
adjustment for 2011-2012 was made to offset the $470 
reduction of $100 per pupil if di stricts adopt four of five 
“Best Practices”.  In order to qualify for the $100 per 
pupil, districts must   
1. Charge employees at least 10% of their health 
care premiums; 
2. Be the “policy holder” of employee medical benefit 
plans; 
3. Develop and implement a service consolidation 
plan; 
4. Obtain competitive bids on non-instructional 
services; and  
5. Provide a dashboard or re port card with specific 
indicators.   
Mr. Rajter also reported that to date 27 bills tied to 
education have been sent to the House and 23 to the 
Senate.  One of bills of gr eat concern, he shared, is 
regarding the reduction/elimination of the personal 
property tax and the effect it would have on funding.   
to backfill the dollars lost in relation to the elimination of 
the personal property tax.  Mr. Rajter stated that the State 
has no obligation – there woul d be no replacement for it 
and the money would be permanently gone.  Ms. 
Philippart asked if it would affect ISD funding as well and 
Mr. Rajter confirmed that it would be county-wide.   Ms. 
Philippart thanked Mr. Rajter for his informative 
presentation.

a letter her students sent to the Governor of Alaska about 
a problem they have had with their pipelines freezing and 
losing oil.  Her students brainstormed ideas and sent them 
to the governor.  The Governor sent a letter in response 
along with pins and pencils from the Alyeska Pipeline 
Service Company.  Dr. Hall provided a copy of the 
Governor’s letter to each Board member.  She also 
mentioned that changes are being made in No Child Left 
Behind and Adequate Yearly Progress may possibly be set 
aside and that it is important to understand how nutrition 
relates to learning and that proteins are needed.  Candy 
and carbs should be eaten later and studies have shown 
that mints help students do better on tests.   
Ms. Philippart thanked Dr. Hall and stated that her 
students must be very excited to have received a response 
back from the Governor of Alaska. 
Jennifer Hilzinger, TSD parent and member of Diversity & 
Inclusion Council, addressed the Board regarding the 
District decision to allow pa rents the right to opt-out of 
watching President Obama’s “Back to School” address to 
students.  She stated that she brought a copy of the letter 
she wrote to the District administration along with 26 
signatures from 51 families who represent 105 Troy 
School District students.  She shared her feelings on how 
the District should lead by setting an example to the 
students and stated that she fe lt it was a partisan political 
act and asked the District to clarify what their intention 
was and to keep politics outside of the school setting.  She 
proceeded to read her letter to the Board. 
Ms. Philippart commented on this subject since the District 
reported receiving several resp onses regarding the issue.  
Dr. Fowler stated that she wanted to read the email 
response that was sent to Ms. Hilzinger later that 
afternoon, which addressed her concerns.   
Joseph Thilman, TSD parent, addressed the Board 
regarding President Obama’s speech to students.  He 
initially stated that he wasn’t aware that the District was 
going to discuss the issue more fully during tonight’s 
meeting.  He added that his concern also was that this 
was a political issue.  His concern was that the message 
being sent to students and parents appeared to be 
political.   
Steve Dunneback, Costello parent, addressed the Board 
regarding the start/end times of schools.  In an effort to 
have more consistency he asked if the District could 
possibly keep the early schools early and late schools with

late starts for the following year. 
Dr. Fowler responded to Mr. Dunneback that this issue 
was revisited several times and the other schools with late 
times want to switch to early times.  The District decided 
on a 5 year plan so that parents don’t have to keep 
switching every couple years with child care issues, jobs, 
transportation, etc.

7. CURRICULUM  
B.  REPORT:  Changes in MEAP and MME  
In the coming months and years we are goin g to see significant changes to our state 
assessment system.  This year  we will be seeing an incr ease in the cut scores for 
proficiency on the MEAP and MME tests.  Th is increase in the cu t score is meant to 
more accurately reflect whet her or not a student is on track for career and college 
readiness.  Additionally, in 2015, we will first administer the Smarter Balance 
computer adaptive assessments measuring the Common Core State Standards.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by Mrs. Anne Mull, 
Troy School District’s Director of Instruction and Assessment.

A. REPORT:   Facilities Study Update 
The following is an overview of the District’s facilities study process and 
introduction of our official Facilities Study Team, which is comprised of 18 
community members.  This study will  take place over the next several 
months and result in a recommendation that will be presented to the Board 
at the February 7, 2012 Board of Education Meeting.  The District would like 
to thank the individuals on the study team for their willingness to participate 
in this important undertaking.       
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report that will be given by  co-chairs Greg Bryen and Waqar 
Hashim.
